ParlaMint-IT_2013-10-16-LEG17-Senato-sed-126.ana.u4	The next item is the continuation of the debate on the draft Constitutional Act No 813-B, which was first approved by the Senate and the House of Deputies.
ParlaMint-IT_2013-10-16-LEG17-Senato-sed-126.ana.u5	Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, yesterday I listened carefully to Mr Quagliariello's report in this House: interesting, very open, in some ways perhaps excessively open, when the government should at least have defined not a preferential lane, but at least a corridor within which to give the Senate Assembly a clearer working hypothesis, within a clearer framework. I share the Minister's reflection when he said that the institutions are not third: through institutions of a certain kind we have the possibility of building a political-institutional system, a socio-economic scenario of a certain type; in front of different institutions the scenario could and can change. I find myself completely and perfectly in disagreement, however, when the Government, through its Minister, argues that the reason why Italy has failed to achieve a form of mature bipolarism is linked to the mere fact that the parties have collapsed. I would add two factors: they are two additional factors, one national and the other national and European, which tell us a lot about the difficulty in Italy of creating a system that has characteristics such as these. The first reason is that, unlike what is happening in the rest of Europe, we have chosen not the redistribution of wealth and the different forms of democracy to look at carefully, but justice as the boundary between the right and the left. Until we eliminate justice as a border point, as a border line between A and B, between right and left, between conservatives and progressives, we will not be in a position to create a normal scenario. The other difference - and this difference must be given a name and a surname - is the fact that in Italy (and elsewhere it is not so or not so in the Italian national measure) there are now only, in addition to the real parties, personal parties. We have reversed the English criterion if there is no party there is no leader and we have replaced it with the principle there is only one party provided that there is a leader. I would add an issue, which is detrimental to bipolarism in Italy and which begins to be so in Europe, which has now chosen bipolarism not only as a way, but also as an orientation since the immediate second post-war period. Europe now lives not only on the reformist right-hand track, but on the rather rooted scenario of extreme left radicalisms or xenophobic radicalisms, populisms and demagogies of various kinds, which permanently collect a consensus by which they effectively oblige the system to reorganise itself in the form of governments that we can define as necessity or emergency. This is what happened in Italy, what happened in Austria and what could happen in Germany. This question, ladies and gentlemen, poses a number of questions not only to the Government, but to both sides of this House, (in fact to the third parties in this House), and above all to the coalition to which I belong. I am referring to the three questions that very often the left has failed to decline: how is globalisation? How is the issue of direct democracy addressed, since the Italian and European left was born within representative and parliamentary democracy? How do you live without parties (and, therefore, how can new parties be refounded)? President Lanzillotta, there has been a multi-firm motion in the Presidency for months - it bears the signature of many elected socialists, of some friends and colleagues of the Democratic Party, perhaps also of the Mixed-SEL Group and beyond - to transpose a recommendation from the European Commission linked to the forthcoming European elections. It is planned to write inside the ballot both the house of European membership to which the parties would like to join (and it must be told the electoral citizens before the elections are held) and the candidate for the presidency of the European Commission that they intend to support. To be fair, Madam President, this motion must be put to the vote. In fact, we cannot support outside, on the streets, that we agree to define an institutional orientation of a certain kind and in the parliamentary classrooms we behave in a decidedly opposite way. Three points and I shall finish. As far as the electoral law is concerned, it is well known that it can carry the form of government: I personally prefer an electoral law that gives citizens the power to choose again, otherwise we should explain why the Constitution continues the diction of the "equal, free and secret" vote. Secondly, I share Mr Quagliariello's working hypothesis when he says that we must reform the perfect bicameralism and we must do so by thinking of a Chamber of self-government, a reduction in the number of elected and a specificity of functions. Thirdly, it is necessary to rethink the role of the parties and to decline Article 49 of the Constitution in the manner of Calamandrei. As we will also be discussing funding, at least public funding for party activity and support for election campaigns, we need to be able to combine these two factors. Only parties reformed and in accordance with democratic rules of freedom can enjoy public funding; if they are not, they cannot enjoy public funding. We will have excellent examples, also in this House, of parties that, not by their own will but by their own standards, should give up funding because they are parties that have absolutely nothing to do with democracy. [[Applause from Senators Buemi and Gambaro.]] The last question concerns local order. It's our responsibility. If we pretend to close the game - and I concluded - abolishing the Provinces without knowing who goes to what, who does what, what does the right hand and if you remember to say it to the left hand, we create a terrible wound in the local Italian institutional system. It makes sense to dissolve the Provinces if we strengthen the Municipalities, if we dissolve the mountain communities, if we go towards the exploitation of the mergers of local authorities, if we review the role, by now bastard, of the Italian regions. To leave the abolition of the Provinces only at the scream of the square, without thinking about what substitute or supplementary order to have in mind, means to create the conditions to rebomb institutions that since 1992 are largely lacking. [[Applause from the Aut Groups (SVP, UV, PATT, UPT) - PSI-MAIE and PD and the Senators D'Onghia, Candiani and Gambaro.]]
ParlaMint-IT_2013-10-16-LEG17-Senato-sed-126.ana.u6	Senator Crimi is enlisted to speak. You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2013-10-16-LEG17-Senato-sed-126.ana.u7	Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, we are once again called upon to debate and vote on this abnormal violation - in our view - of fundamental principles of a state that aspires to call itself democratic. We are called upon to pronounce on a bill that changes the basic rules, no, even worse: derogation from the rules to change the basic rules, that is, the Constitution. We must remember that. For months we have been discussing how to amend the Constitution: not the contents of the possible amendment, but only the ways in which to amend it. A first question arises in this regard: is there really a willingness to amend the Constitution? If so, why did not the debate on the substance begin immediately? Yes, perhaps there is the will on the part of this majority not only to change it, but to turn it upside down, bend it and shape it in its own interests, that is quite clear; but it is also clear that the debate on reforms has been taken away from Parliament. It was taken away (and it took time) as it was necessary to give time to this assembly of essays (attribute important enough to define people who certainly have technical skills, but wisdom is a great virtue) to formulate a theory. So what we've been doing for a few months has been wasting time working outside Parliament, totally outsourcing the debate on reforms, but in this waste of time, a change in the rules had to be adopted to speed up the next debate. Parliament should be the privileged forum in which to build a serious debate, but this - as we have already seen - does not happen and is not possible. This Parliament has been deprived of its role as a place of representation of the people: we see it every day with the mortification that we must suffer, crushed by the bullies of a government that uses the exceptional instrument of the decree-law (it should be an exceptional instrument, which should have the characteristics of necessity, urgency and homogeneity) as an ordinary mode of legislative action. It is a few days ago the example of the decree-law on gender violence, so-called feminicide: the theme of feminicide was used to ride a compelling social theme, used as a Trojan horse, a moral blackmail that led us to have to approve that package penalty the decline of the positive consequences of only five articles on gender violence, bringing in all the other garbage. However, one doubt remains: the government - we have repeated it several times - should not be entitled to influence any constitutional changes or reforms; it should be a task of Parliament. But perhaps not even this Parliament is legitimate: it has been elected by an electoral law - which, I remember, you have been keeping alive for eight years - which everyone considered illegitimate, yet the right to amend the fundamental Charter governing our democratic life is being arrogant. We are not amending the Constitution on this basis. First of all, it is an illegitimate Parliament, as we have said several times: a Parliament, appointed by party secretariats, elected by an electoral law that risks receiving a judgment of unconstitutionality. It is an illegitimate government, born from the will of the Innominable, and I leave it to you to imagine who it is. There is a group of wise men who legitimized with a pseudo-wisdom signature the government's will to launch constitutional reforms: not the will of Parliament, but the will of the government. All this by way of derogation from the guarantee rules laid down in the Constitutional Charter itself. A Parliament, this Parliament, which should approve this derogation, which should approve constitutional reforms, composed of people who vote using a paper ball instead of their presence. [[Applause from the M5S Group]] Is this Parliament that should approve the fundamental reforms of the Constitutional Charter? Is this Parliament, made up of people who defraud the state? And these people are supposed to approve a constitutional reform? All this is unlawful. Then I want to speak on the report by Minister Quagliariello, according to which for the government constitutional reforms are the central hub of the program, thus confirming that it is the Government that pulls the strings of the operation. The Minister speaks of the fragility of the institutional system and blames the parties, without which, in his opinion, the democratic system would not be conceivable. However, and at the same time, he says that the parties have failed, that they have collapsed, that they are now in decline: the Minister says so, we do not say so. At this point, however, if the parties are gradually losing their role, I have the doubt that we are not in one democratic state, because either. The minister then talks about the defense of bipolarism. But the bipolarism of which many people fill our mouths is already there at this moment and is clear: on the one hand the Democratic Party and the People of Freedom and on the other the 5 Star Movement. Bipolarism exists: there is a majority made up of PD and PdL together and an opposition, the only opposition that perhaps has not been seen in Parliament for some time, that is, ours. This is the reality: bipolarism has been achieved by citizens in fact by their vote. The Minister also spoke of strengthening the powers of the Government (therefore the decree of urgency is not enough) and of overlapping competences. But who voted for that overlap of competences? Who voted for the previous rules that led the country to this situation regarding local autonomy? This same Parliament. The wise men may have forgotten to examine, because there is no form of government in the report: the presidential monarchy, the monarchy of the President of the Republic, the monarchy of those who decide and influence parliamentary activity, as our President of the Republic is currently doing, even today, again, with the warnings. This is what's happening. You have spoken of the difference between the cost of political activities and the cost of democracy. It is for this reason that the electoral refunds are still there. It is for this reason that they have simply changed their names, now becoming two times a thousand, now becoming something else, but they always stay there and keep them always. This is the situation we are in. The country does not need institutional reforms right now. You're looking for a scapegoat. You're looking for an excuse in the instrument. When a driver makes an accident, he doesn't condemn the car but the driver. When you use a weapon, you don't condemn the weapon but the one who uses it and uses it badly. That is the reality. We must not condemn the instrument: we must condemn those who in recent decades have been inadequate in using it to solve the problems of the country. As I said last time, I imagine every morning a housewife, a retired person, a precarious person or an unemployed person who wakes up and his first thought in the morning, even before taking the children to school or going to the employment office to look for a job, before even dealing with the bills, is that of presidentialism: if presidentialism yes or presidentialism no, whether perfect bicameralism or monocameralism. I imagine all Italian citizens who every morning have only this fixed thought: institutional reforms! That is not the case, ladies and gentlemen. It's not like that, Minister. At this moment the country needs reforms, but serious reforms, in support of small and medium-sized enterprises, of those who do not arrive at the end of the month, of those who do not have work. Citizenship income and support for small businesses: these are the only reforms that the country needs right now. And let's still remember the income of citizenship, an income of dignity. This is the only true reform that the country needs: the total revolution of the Italian welfare system. Certainly not the change of the perfect bicameralism or presidentialism, these are not the things with which you arrive at the end of the month and with which you eat every day! [[Applause from the M5S Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2013-10-16-LEG17-Senato-sed-126.ana.u9	Madam President, Minister, ladies and gentlemen, we are here today once again discussing constitutional reforms as if the country's salvation depended on them, as if the Italians every day considered the transformation of the government as the solution to all their problems. But the question we have to ask ourselves today in this House is one: do Italian citizens really ask us this? We have unemployment at 12%, youth unemployment at 40%, 15 million Italians live in a condition of deprivation and economic hardship, we have a school dropout that has reached unprecedented percentages, a decrease in consumption and the purchasing power of wages and the other European countries do not envy us at all. As many as 12,442 companies closed in 2012, adding the 4,218 that delivered their books to court in the first quarter of this year. These are the priorities that this House must address, but according to the reasoning of the Government and the majority the introduction of the direct election of the Head of State or the strengthening of the powers of the Prime Minister would allow Italian citizens to face the day serenely. Why do you come to tell us that Italy has run the risk of failing for the failed constitutional reforms and you do not tell us about the monstrous debt accumulated? What are we talking about? The problem, ladies and gentlemen, is that the economic approach to the reform of the Constitution is now a mantra of this Government, and so much so that it has included constitutional reforms in the DEF Update Note that we approved last week. This is not participation. Too often it is confused and limited to its first stage, information. True participation is when citizens can direct our choices as politicians, they can ask for and get the change of wrong and harmful choices. Parliament should be the first representative body of these wills, but all too often it is unheard of, as has been the case in this House for the reform of judicial geography, and it aligns itself, bending to the will of the Government: both conform, without room for proper rethinking. Where is the respect for the citizens? You failed them all. We want to restore the role of popular sovereignty, we want to improve the democratic approach of the Constitution. Who despite his common sense wants to be remembered for this vote in favour of this bill. It's a political choice. You'll take responsibility. If you wish, however, to be remembered for giving life to change and having had the courage to respond to your conscience against your party and not your party, you will vote against and recover with us the value of a true democracy and a Constitution saved and implemented! ParlaMint-IT_2013-10-16-LEG17-Senato-sed-126.ana.u10	He is registered to speak Senator Barozzino. You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2013-10-16-LEG17-Senato-sed-126.ana.u11	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, today I will bring to this House a document and my thoughts and most of the citizens: The main street is the manifesto of the assembly for the Constitution, which saw on 12 October a great demonstration of ordinary people. ==References====External links== Faced with the miseries, personal ambitions and rivalries of groups dealing with state affairs, we invite citizens - and I would add politicians - not to be distracted. We invite them to question the great problems of our society and to rediscover politics and its compass: the Constitution. The dignity of people, social justice and solidarity with the weak and marginalized, legality and the abolition of privileges, fairness in the distribution of the burdens and sacrifices imposed by the economic crisis, the hope of freedom, work and culture for the younger generations, justice and democracy in Europe, peace: this is the Constitution. The defence of the Constitution is not a tired reference to a text written many years ago. This is not an absurd conservative attitude, out of date. Do we not have, today more than ever, in the daily lives of so many people, a need for dignity, legality, justice and freedom? Do we not need Constitution-oriented policy? Do we not need a deep reclamation in the name of the principles and democratic participation it lays down? On the other hand, the idea that this Constitution is out of date has made its way, not by chance and not innocently; that it prevents the modernization of our country; that individual and collective rights are a brake on economic development; that solidarity is an empty word; that the dramas and desperation of individuals and families are an inevitable price to pay; that political participation and Parliament are obstacles; that the government should only be the efficiency of economic policy at the service of investors; that the true constitution is, therefore, another: both the Diktat of the markets to which everything else must be subordinated. In a word: the idea has emerged that democracy has made its time and that it is now in a post-democratic time: the time to replace the government of the economic and financial "technical" government with the government of the democratic "political." Thus, the "inescapable reforms" - as they have been defined - are explained, inescapable in order to move from one Constitution to another. The defence of the Constitution is therefore first and foremost the promotion of an idea of society, which differs from that of those who have worked so far tacitally to empty it and, now, operate to tamper formally with it. It is a commitment, at the same time, cultural and political, that calls for a clear definition of the nature of the stakes and for the gathering of as many forces as possible to reach and mobilize. It is not the defence of a past that cannot return, but a programme for a future to be built in Italy and Europe. 2. Yet, no matter what has been done to remove it from the official political discourse in which it was evoked only because of the will to change it, the Constitution has been alive in recent years. Today, we are aware of the topicality of Article 1 of the Constitution, which places the work at the base, at the foundation of democracy: an article long devalued or mocked as an expression of empty ideology. Today, we rediscover the value of equality, as a requirement for justice and strength of social cohesion, according to the proclamation of art. 3 of the Constitution: an article long considered an antique and replaced by the praise of inequality and unlimited competition on the social scale. Today, the dignity of the person and the inviolability of his fundamental rights, proclaimed by art. 2 of the Constitution, represent the defense against the commodification of the life of human beings, according to the "natural" laws of the market. Today, tax duty and tax fairness, according to the criterion of progressive participation in public expenditure, proclaimed by art. 53 of the Constitution, it proves to be an essential cornerstone of every possible bond of citizenship, after so many years of tolerance, or even of justification and praise, of tax evasion. Here, with some examples, what is the idea of a just society that the Constitution indicates to us. In recent years, the defence of essential rights, such as the management of common goods, the guarantee of trade union rights, the protection of motherhood, the self-determination of people at critical moments of existence, has taken place in the name of the Constitution, more in the classrooms of the courts than in those of Parliament; and we must ask ourselves about this - more in popular mobilizations than in legislative and government initiatives. On the contrary, we can see that the Constitution, the more it has been ignored at the top, the more it has become a reference point for so many people, movements, associations in civil society (...). In short: while the official public space was lost in an increasingly senseless power game and emptied itself of constitutional sense, it came alongside a wider informal public space, occupied by spontaneous forces. Roads and squares have offered extraordinary opportunities for encounter and mutual recognition. They must continue to be so, because there the political novelty has taken on strength and communication capacity; there, for a few moments, isolation and loneliness have been overcome; there a different society has been imagined. There, the word of the Constitution has resounded completely naturally (...). I have heard many times in this House names such as Berlinguer, Pertini. Enrico Berlinguer - I say this to that political part that is identified there - said that a leftist politician does things for the people and together with the people. To want to be good - I of course do not believe it - these reforms are made for the people, but they are certainly not done together with the people, since they were neither informed nor involved. Finally, I respond to those who say that the subscribers of this document are conservatives (Landini and Rodotà). I, who, of course, feel very close to these men, say that if I defend the Constitution, and therefore a worthy democracy, if I do not condemn the citizens to find themselves taking off their cup to work - because we are talking about this - it means being conservative, then I am very happy to be a conservative; indeed, I am proud of it. I conclude with a sentence by Sandro Pertini, who has also been referred to this House several times. Pertini said that if a government does not do what the people want it must be thrown out, even with clubs, if necessary. I invite you to reflect on this. [[Applause from the Mixed-SEL and M5S Groups and Senator Borioli]]

Our Constitutional Charter has already shown its strength and nobility since its simplicity. All but all Italian citizens can read and understand it. The rights and duties are clear, clear. When it was written it came out of the nightmare of war and the Resistance. ParlaMint-IT_2013-10-16-LEG17-Senato-sed-126.ana.u17	Madam President, at one of the first meetings I had the opportunity to attend, we were talking about this very issue and there was the usual deserted House. Clearly, certain issues are not of interest to a political side. For a long time now, an attempt has been made to change the meaning of the Constitution. All the interventions that we have witnessed so far seek to declassify the fundamental law of the State from the primary instrument of democracy to the instrument of guarantee of the few, often forgetting above all that the Constitutional Charter was born in order to guarantee equality, social justice, transparency of power and responsibility of those who govern the country, that is the fundamental characteristics of a democracy. It therefore seems that we are going in the direction of an oligarchical change, represented by a regime of inequality and privileges that conceal a power made of connivances and clientele, not only at national, but also at international level. Continuing on this path, your much announced and necessary reform will only be yet another counter-reform and yet another internal damage to democracy and will only increase the distance between citizens and political representatives. But we're not in! We will use all the tools in our possession to guarantee our Constitution and to prevent the misuse that you are making of it and if many of us representatives of the 5 Star Movement Group have signed up to speak, it is because this is the tool that we have and use it. A famous constitutionalist, Professor Gustavo Zagrebelsky, states: Above all, who wants to change the Constitution should be asked: what is the mandate that authorizes you? The constituent power does not belong to you at all. You have been elected - we have been elected - to stand below, not above the Constitution. If you claim to be above, you lack legitimacy, you are usurpers. If we really don't want to use big words, let's say you're like the frog trying to swell up to become an ox. This isn't the first time. It's already happened. But does that mean that what is illegitimate has therefore become legitimate? The Constitution belongs to everyone. That being said, this does not mean that it is not editable, but must not become your refuge. It is not the Constitution that has to stand on the bench of the accused, but the current conception of politics and a large part of the ruling class which, instead of renewing itself, pretends to impose its conception of politics and the myriad of underlying interests. We of the 5 Star Movement are not in principle opposed to change, indeed it is clear that the broad electoral consensus at the last political elections emphasizes that the people call for a profound renewal and asked for it loudly. And we represent all the voices of the citizens who long for this change. The point, however, is that the only and necessary condition for taking this path is to do so in the forms and means of democracy. Here, however, we are faced with a continuous failure of politics. [[Applause from Senator Airola.]] We stayed silent, you looked at us, you insulted us, but you applauded this failure and what you are doing now is a further testimony. We will strive in this sense by fighting any form of illegality, immunity, privilege and the dark sides of power, in order to pursue truth and justice, by reducing inequalities in order to rebalance society, by guaranteeing rights to the weakest, those same weak ones who, due to the devastating economic crisis, are increasingly marginalized and left alone by the State. It is a question of whether these constitutional reforms, such as the reduction in the number of Members of Parliament, the much-pronounced elimination of the Provinces, the revision of bicameralism, the new form of government, are hiding more ready to satisfy those interests of which I have spoken before, again to the detriment of the citizens. Wouldn't adopting a semi-presidency system as a form of government be a counter-reform rather than a reform? It is not the idea of participatory democracy that is foreseen in our Constitution, but the investiture of one man, bearer and guarantor of a series of established interests. The procedure for revising the Constitution (Article 138) already exists. But now we are debating another procedure, undoubtedly forced, which does not belong to the parliamentary assemblies, thus circumventing ordinary procedures, and precisely this forcing is the manifest manifestation of the inability of the current political class. ParlaMint-IT_2013-10-16-LEG17-Senato-sed-126.ana.u18	Senator Bertorotta is registered to speak. You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2013-10-16-LEG17-Senato-sed-126.ana.u19	Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, the constitutional bill under the heading 'Institution of the Parliamentary Committee for Constitutional Reforms' has arrived in this House after the very sweaty scrutiny and even sanctioned in terms of suspending the work of the House, as was the case in the other branch of Parliament, which saw a non-obstructional struggle of the 5 Star Movement, but a "constructionist" struggle to defend the article establishing the procedures for amending the Constitutional Charter. In order to understand the intellectual and political debate, interwoven in recent months around this constitutional arrangement, perhaps we should start from its very content. And indeed Article 138 states: The laws of revision of the Constitution and other constitutional laws are adopted by each Chamber with two successive deliberations at an interval of not less than three months, and are approved by an absolute majority of the members of each Chamber in the second vote. The laws themselves are subject to popular referendum when, within three months of their publication, one fifth of the members of a House, five hundred thousand voters or five regional councils apply. The law submitted to a referendum shall not be enacted unless it is approved by a majority of valid votes. A referendum shall not take place if the law has been passed in the second vote by each of the Chambers by a two-thirds majority of its members. Therefore, to modify or derogate from it would be to open the way for the much-discussed reforms. You will all remember that already in summer the battle on Article 138 of the Constitution filled the front pages of the newspapers and not only. In defense of Article 138, in fact, the 5 Star Movement occupied Montecitorio in order to communicate to the public what was happening and above all what changes were going on under the table and to the unawares of the voters were making to our Constitutional Charter. Precisely because the voters were unaware of everything, the Movement decided to restore sovereignty to the citizens, giving them the opportunity to choose their representatives and to revoke them in case they do not comply with the mandate or in case of serious failure. The political task for us is a service to citizenship, temporary, and not a tool to build a managerial career for enrichment purposes. In order to understand our battle, perhaps we should make a clarification that I am sure will not be so obscure: the Constitution of the Italian Republic is a rigid text. What does this mean? That is hierarchically placed above ordinary law and can be modified only by constitutional laws. There are also specific limits to the constitutional revision, including Article 138, which defines norms and times, and Article 139, which defines the republican nature of our country as unchangeable, just as the first twelve articles containing fundamental principles are considered unchangeable. Article 138 provides that the laws amending the Constitution and other constitutional laws shall be adopted by each Chamber with two successive votes at intervals not less than three months (two for the Chamber and two for the Senate, crossed). No qualified majority is required for the first vote, so constitutional law can also be adopted by a simple majority; absolute majority is required in the second vote. In the event that the law is not passed by at least two thirds, it is possible to submit it to a referendum of a confirmation type, without quorum, if one fifth of the members of a Chamber or 500,000 voters or five regional councils so request. The law submitted to a referendum shall not be enacted unless it is approved by a majority of valid votes. It is obvious that we want to put our hands on the instruments of revision of the Constitution so that we can change it radically and quickly. The reason behind this provision is therefore that the Constitution is the cause of the decline of the ruling classes. Instead, it is the exact opposite: we are all witnesses of the progressive decline of politics, so much so that we want to upset the Constitution. However, driven by an impeccable extra-ordinam procedure, we have come to a far-reaching constitutional reform, but here it seems that there is only what remains of a bicycle to pedal to distant lands, to lands where citizens can express themselves as they did after the Second World War. But let's move on. The government's remaining office is linked to the achievement of this goal within a set deadline, and not to the resolution of the political nodes of greatest concern for the citizens, that is, the way out of this damn economic and social crisis. But this is not urgent for the majority; it is urgent. The Commission's stated aim, its only policy point, is to derogate from Article 138 in order to approve, very quickly, an extraordinary revision of the Charter from its very foundation. After all, we're seeing surgery, we're amputating our country's legs, and who knows what we're gonna do again. It is not a matter of restructuring, on which the 5 Star Movement would have the will and perhaps also the pleasure of confronting the majority, in the wake of the lines drawn by the constituent Fathers. The idea of the government and the majority who support it is that of a demolition, a reconstruction of the entire institutional architecture, and this is demonstrated by three elements. Meanwhile, the central role in the reform process is not at the two chambers, but at the Committee and at the Government. Then, the timing, particularly tight of the schedule, which must be achieved within the peremptory period of eighteen months, with all the possible consequences on the function of Parliament, now downgraded to mere beautiful statue. Nobody wants to admit it, but what we are trying to do at the moment is to restrict the political rights of the parliamentary minority forces to a minimum. We understand that, ladies and gentlemen. Not to mention that the critical profiles found in the measure are many, and do not concern only the constitutional law: in many cases they affect and even violate the principles and procedural rules laid down by the parliamentary regulations. For example, the Committee is given powers and functions by way of derogation from the parliamentary regulations themselves, thus placing it in an over-ordinate position vis-à-vis Parliament itself. No one, apart from us in the minority, admits that this draft law tramples on the most basic principles of democratic representation, which are all the more important to safeguard in the composition of a Committee that wants to assume the prerogative of amending the Constitution. Or rather, no one says that the famous Committee will incarnate the souls of our constituent Fathers, giving itself a sort of power of self-regulation. The 5 Star Movement is radically opposed, both to the constitutional revision procedure and to the object of the same, as outlined by this provision. The derogation from the rule governing the process of revising the Constitution turns this Parliament into an impotent constituent assembly. Not to mention that the same sovereignty, which belongs to the people, must be exercised in the forms and limits established by the Constitution. On the other hand, what we are witnessing, and it is inconceivable, is that this principle should be derogated from by a restricted Committee chosen by political representatives appointed by the party secretariats. It's just a pretext. It is only a way of surreptitiously covering the void of the political atrophy of this bipolarism, which everything says, except to be perfect. The most angry thing is that illustrious university teachers, experts in law, have declared that this is a real illegal disapplication of Article 138, in order to make changes to the Constitution without the necessary respect of this rule. But how can we propose a derogation from Article 138? The norm is that which in fact guarantees our Constitutional Charter, the wall of containment of the arbitrariness of political power, especially that of the majorities of government. The rigidity of our Constitution is a good and just thing. It is worth remembering that it was precisely the flexibility of the Albertino Statute that allowed the transition to dictatorship by legal means. This attempt to circumvent the Article 138 procedure is constitutionally unlawful, as it violates fundamental principles which are taken away from the same parliamentary power. The current draft law in fact constitutes an extraordinary and exceptional procedure for the constitutional text, both from a procedural point of view and from the point of view of the bodies involved in amending the Republican Constitution. Of course we in the 5 Star Movement have not so far defended the Constitution without admitting that it needs some changes, but it is certain that the latter must take place in compliance with the rules. The 5 Star Movement does not understand the reasons that drive the majority to go towards presidentialism, that is, more power to the President of the Council, with further emptying of sense and power of Parliament, which would become a mere body of ratification. But then the criticism that has moved, and even today moves, the Movement starts from a question: do you explain to me what the Parliament will need after the adoption of this law? Why don't we get up and go home? The 5 Star Movement does not want to make simple obstructionism, it wants to give centrality back to Parliament, with the clear separation between the legislative power, held precisely by us parliamentarians, and the executive power of the Government, keeping away the judicial power. Parliament must regain its central role in the process of revising our Constitutional Charter. The abandonment of the normal procedure of examination, explicitly provided for in Article 72 of the Constitution for the examination of draft laws in constitutional matters, the granting to the Government of a privileged amending power, the impossibility for individual parliamentarians to submend the proposals of the Government or of the Committee, the prohibition for parliamentarians in disagreement with their Groups to present their sub-amendments, the derogations provided for in the Chamber and Senate Regulations constitute as many choices that humiliate and compress the autonomy and freedom of the parliamentarians and therefore the role and legislative function of the Parliament. Maybe we need to read the book XI de "The spirit of the laws" of Montesquieu, where we trace the theory of the separation of powers. Article 138 of the Constitution remains the last bulwark against anti-institutional drifts and to defend democracy and our future. I close my speech with the words of Don Luigi Sturzo, who I hope can torment your minds at the time of the vote, indeed I hope they can take away your sleep if necessary: The Constitution is the foundation of the Republic. If it falls from the heart of the people, if it is not respected by the political authorities, if it is not defended by the Government and by Parliament, if it is tampered with by the parties, the hard ground on which our institutions are manufactured and anchored our freedoms will fail. [[Applause from the M5S Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2013-10-16-LEG17-Senato-sed-126.ana.u57	Mr President, I believe that there is a preliminary question that needs to be clarified, having listened to most of the speeches that have taken place so far. There is a kind of confessional vision, a fidelistic vision of our Constitution, which must obviously be refuted: this is not a law written by God, but by men; we must obviously relativize the value of a law, even though it is fundamental, such as the Constitution. Now, if we are legislators - if it is completely inappropriate, because of course we are - there is no doubt that we must select the most relevant circumstances in the context of our society. The legislator's work progresses if the legislator is able to understand what is really important, in relation to the needs that characterize a certain historical moment. The Senate will forgive me for the absolutely concise and rapid nature of my exposition, because to evoke all the phases of our most recent history that have determined the needs before us today would obviously be too challenging from the point of view of the times, which must inevitably be reduced. In short, the crisis of politics, the lack of credibility of politics are particularly marked on the side of a historical issue, already when the so-called first Republic existed: the inefficiency of Parliament, the general inefficiency of the State, the need to have political majorities that are stable over time, that are also certain, in order to allow the necessary orientation of the electorate towards one or the other politically feasible option. Even the rule of alternation, on which all of us rightly dwell, inevitably implies the need to be able to have a political majority that, over the five years normally destined for the course of the legislature, can try to implement a political program that otherwise would remain entrusted to too short time spaces and inevitably could not even be implemented; so, speaking of alternation obviously means exercising something absolutely theoretical, without any practical feasibility. We are then faced with a complex of needs that distinguish the need to modernise our constitutional structure and our institutional relations, by virtue of the need to guarantee citizens, thus recovering with the electorate that relationship of trust and credibility that makes politics strong, a panorama of interventions we could say reformers of our Constitution. On the other hand, we must think very carefully about what constitutes the dominant value of our present Constitution, with reference to the part of the constitutional dictation that we intend to amend today. There is no doubt that what we would like to change, namely the perfect bicameralism, the number of parliamentarians, the form of government, obeys, in the current constitutional order, what in 1947, therefore after fascism and after the Second World War, constituted an absolutely important value, that is, to guarantee a system of controls so exasperated that it makes impossible an authoritarian, if not totalitarian, drift of the State. That was the goal of 1947. If, then, we are legislators, if we are to identify the relevant element in the context not only of our society, but also of the demands that arise at such a dramatic moment on the economic side, making it necessary to streamline parliamentary procedures, ensuring that legislative texts are adopted more quickly, we must inevitably agree that the prevailing value in 1947, namely the control of one House over another, of one power over another, this rigorous balance of powers, has become something that no longer corresponds to current needs and needs. Today those risks of authoritarian or totalitarian involution of the State are completely unimaginable, so we are facing constitutional values that are inevitably set. We could say that those constitutional values were inevitably transitory, because they obeyed the contingency of a historical moment that has now finally passed, I would say now with reference to an appreciable period of time, if it is true that these requirements of modernising our Constitution on this limited side were already affirmed when the so-called first Republic was alive. On the other hand, Senators, it is inevitable that there is a reflection here on a circumstance, on which, however, it would seem distracted the attention of those who protest for a modification, like the one desired, of the current constitutional structure: here no one thinks of modifying the fundamental values on which the Constitution is based. In contrast to the conclusions, a newly intervened senator spoke of the first 12 articles of the Constitution such as those containing the so-called hard clog, at least defined as such by our most current constitutionalists, namely those norms that contain the form, republican, of the State, the foundation of our Republic at work today, inevitably, its solidaristic inspiration, the attention to the world of work through the desired intervention of precautionary norms that make that of work a truly peaceful context, the protection of the fundamental values of the human person, not only as an individual, but also within society and in the intermediate space within the organizations that stand out between society and individuals, in which these fundamental rights must be realized. Here there is no talk of modifying the obligation contained in Article 3, which the Constitution and the Republic assume, aimed at removing the obstacles that limit the realization of the human person within society, of all the organizations and intermediate statutes between person and society. There is no question of a very fundamental value here. Today, no one could think of a Constitution that, for example, did not regulate the indispensable principle of equality of citizens before the law, the need to provide for equal situations, regardless of the starting economic position. Certainly the achievement of the final objectives must then be subordinate to the merit of each individual. Having thought, as some have unfortunately done, of an inevitably egalitarian society has depowered our society and has made merit appear irrelevant, leading to a general lowering of the intellectual level; moreover, it is easy to verify only that we inform, read or listen to debates, which seem extremely inattentive precisely with reference to values such as rigour, deepening, intellectual and valuable content of the debate. Today we are proposing a legislative amendment to our Constitution, which, according to the report, to the work that has been read - for those who have obviously read them and have not thought of intervening by slogans, pursuing impossible electoral consensus - aims at the elimination of bicameralism. I believe that there can be no political force that today can approve of a perfect bicameralism like that of which we have suffered so far (I use the participle past not by chance, because it is indicative of the difficulties that stand in the way of the speed of political decisions that must place us at the same level as other European countries). At the same time, as regards the link between electoral law, such as something posterior, form of government and elimination of the perfect bicameralism, we have finally realized that we will never have an electoral law that allows certain and stable majorities if we have not first eliminated this unnecessary duplication of legislative competences. Then there is the proposed reduction in the number of Members of Parliament; here, perhaps, I would be a little more cautious, having now known the complexity of the state mechanism. In short, these are reforms - and I conclude, Mr President - on which I do not believe that anyone can seriously object to anything if we look at the content I am therefore very much in favour of the fact that the machine of our State can actually be aged immediately (then there will be explanations of vote). ParlaMint-IT_2013-10-16-LEG17-Senato-sed-126.ana.u61	Mr President, I am the spokesperson for a document drawn up by the magistrates of the Area, a coordination between magistrates of various components and origins, and - I do not want to be Senator Bisinella, with the kindness with which I always address her - I will speak for about 40 comments that I received between 23 and 24 last night on this subject, which demonstrate our total glue to the citizens, and not neckline. The Co-ordination of Magistrates states: The process of reform of the Constitution undertaken by the Government of the "boundary agreements" and coagulated in the draft constitutional law of government initiative n. 813 of 2013 - establishing the Parliamentary Committee for Constitutional Reforms - raises concern; concern confirmed by the latest statements of the President of the Council, who, in the debate on confidence, re-launched the need to proceed rapidly on the ground of constitutional reforms. Following on from what happened with the establishment of the bicameral Commission of 2007 - Constitutional Law of 24 January 1997, No. 1, which had been hesitated in a failure - once again, the Charter was replaced by a derogation from Article 138 thereof and the configuration of an extraordinary procedure for revising Titles I, II, III and V of Part Two and of the strictly related rules. In the name of an alleged effectiveness of the reform measure and of the urgency imposed by the needs of the economy, the procedural exacerbations sculpted in Article 138 are removed and reduced to a bulwark of the rigidity of the Charter and its patrimony of inviolable rights, social justice and the dimension of the democratic and pluralist state. The dismantling of the procedural guarantees of Article 138 is represented by the reduction of the interval between the first and the second approval by each Chamber - the so-called pause for reflection - from the minimum period of three months to the minimum period of 45 days and is supplemented by the acceleration of the so-called timetable: parliamentary work will have to be organised in such a way that it will be completed within eighteen months of the entry into force of the constitutional law establishing the Committee. These simplifications and accelerations are only partly tempered by the possibility of carrying out the popular referendum even in case of second approval by each House with a two-thirds majority. A process of revision, therefore, - the magistrates point out - which could extend to the radical change of the form of government and which for the moment leaves only the weariness of the principle of rigidity of the Constitution, the attraction of the process of constitutional revision in the orbit of the function of political direction and the development of a dangerous expedient - the derogation from Article 138 - which gives the availability of the Constitution into the hands of the political majority of the moment. The severity of the wound inflicted on the rigidity of the Charter is amplified by the political claim to indissolubly tie the destiny of the Government to the destiny of constitutional reforms and thus condition its outcome. All this in the context of a worrying lack of attention manifested by the whole of the party forces present in Parliament and revealed by the silence with which the appeals were received to individual parliamentarians coming from broad areas of constitutionalism and Italian associationism. It must be reiterated strongly that the principle of rigidity of the Constitution, removed from all changes and guarded by the procedures aggravated by the approval of the laws of revision of Article 138, was not thought of by the constituents to establish the absolute intangibility of the Charter, but to ensure that any revision of the fundamental citizenship pact could take place in a context as similar as possible to the constituent one, including the wider spectrum of political forces - majority and especially minority - and freed from the dominant political orientation. In accordance, moreover, with what happens in countries that are commonly taken as a model of decision-making efficiency of democratic processes, in which the revision of the fundamental law is allowed, but in the context of procedures even more rigid and complex than those sanctioned by Article 138. In the United States the revision is possible in the reduced form of the amendment, the simple proposal of which must be made by two thirds of the members of the Congress and two thirds of the assemblies of the various States. Also in the document of the magistrates we read: In the face of an immemorial policy of the fact that the only successful constitutional reforms were those channeled on the tracks of Article 138, the need that is felt is to make even stronger the guarantee of rigidity of the Charter, in order to protect the Constitution from adventures promoted by political majorities of the moment. All the more so at a time when the composition of political representation is altered by an electoral mechanism that rescinds the link between popular and elected will and risks handing the government over to minority forces. It is necessary to remove the Charter from the blackmail of political majorities and strongly reaffirm that the Constitution is an inalienable heritage of the Italian people. The latter, with the vote against the draft revision of Part II of the Constitution that some 16 million Italians expressed in the referendum of 25 and 26 June 2006, has again elected it as a teacher and pole star of public life and social relations. The defence of the Constitution is not conservation. Others, however, are the desirable reforms that the country needs. Reforms aimed at updating and developing the draft of the fundamental law, but in line with the values of a Constitution, which, especially in the contingent period of economic and social crisis, highlights only one urgent necessity: to be implemented. ParlaMint-IT_2013-10-16-LEG17-Senato-sed-126.ana.u63	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I am pleased with the Minister's presence, but I hope that he will be able to listen carefully not only to my speech but also to future ones, perhaps with a little less attention on the telephone. I would like to tell you, in these few minutes, what the Constitution of the Republic represents for a citizen like me. And I tell you through the words of men and women that the Constitution has written it. I do not forget Piero Calamandei's speech to the students of Milan in 1955. I do not forget this because, in that speech, Calamandrei, referring to the fundamental Charter, never speaks of development, of banks, of economy, of technology; he simply speaks, and with magnificent elocution, of man, of his dignity, of the instinct of solidarity that he must have. It speaks of equality, Calamandrei, of individual and collective rights, of freedom to be, of building one's present and future. Many years have passed since the difficult time when the Constituents, after a long war of hatred, death and destruction, full of enthusiasm and hope, realized the normative principles capable of determining a profound transformation in social reality, starting from a primary value that fascism had unknown: man and his dignity. Out of the experience of the authoritarian regime, the strong intention of the Constituents of all political parties was to guarantee individual rights and denied freedoms. How long has it been since Calamandrei, La Pira, Mortati, and with them all the Catholic, socialist and communist parliamentarians, had no doubts and were really, and not instrumentally, cohesive in recognizing the inviolable rights of man, as stated in Article 2, both as a single person, and in the social formations where his personality takes place? I, on the other hand, am not in Parliament where illustrious and valiant men sat, who designed a world at the centre of human dignity. I am speaking in Parliament farther, by form and substance, from what was Parliament that wrote our Constitution. The Parliament which, most of all in the history of our Republic, disregards the fundamental principles of the wonderful project that is the Constitution. The Parliament in which we discuss daily, and for purposes of massive distraction, banks, development, industry, markets, MIB index; how to save Guy or Caio from jail. A Parliament that is unable to form an anti-Mafia Commission [[Applause from the M5S Group]] and who executes, mechanically, orders from a government plundered and lobbied. It is so, colleagues, and it is amazing to think that you, until now merely execute orders deriving from the Executive, are you who have no title, no merit, no experience of dictatorship, death and pain behind you, to think that you can modify what Calamandrei called a will written with the blood of one hundred thousand dead. A testament of one hundred thousand dead is an immutable document, especially if implemented in its fundamental principles, of freedom, solidarity and equality. This, on the other hand, is Parliament in which a power such as the legislative one, which is itself the basis and the garrison of the Constitution, does not legislate, but allows, in total contempt of its own fundamental law, a shameless and continuous decree of urgency. I ask all of us a great effort, but I ask you to really do so, that is to imagine coming back for a few moments or remembering through the stories of your loved ones, if you have not experienced it directly, the experience of the fascist regime and of war. Each of you will have - I am sure - a memory of an episode of death, of hunger, of deprivation, of humiliation, limitations of every freedom of aggregation, of criticism, of different thought, of annulment of the dignity of men. In remembering it now, you have to focus on the number 138. This, ladies and gentlemen, is the number that protects our Italian Constitution from the possibility of renovating dictatorships. By virtue of Article 138, our Constitution is in fact a "rigid" Constitution, which can be modified only by a constitutional law, and this is not by a will or a whim, but - be careful - for the ease that Fascism had to limit and then suppress the rights and freedoms that the Albertino Statute also recognized. To state, as some politicians who are particularly intolerant of democratic rules feel, that the process of amending the Constitution provided for in Article 138 is exceptionally difficult and burdensome, and should therefore be wisely lightened, is false. [[Applause from the M5S Group]] Far more difficult are other Constitutions: in the United States, for example, in addition to the vote of the two branches of Congress, the positive vote of two thirds of the Member States is also required. In Switzerland the double vote of the same Assembly is not enough: after the approval of the reform law, the House that approved it must be dissolved and new elections must be held; to approve for the second time the constitutional reform will then be the House exited from the elections in its renewed composition. Our article 138 is far less demanding, yet it is to be weakened with disarming superficiality. I would also point out the absurd history of a rigid Constitution which, if attacked by illegitimate laws, should be defended by the Constitutional Court whose judges are appointed by the President of the Republic. I smile bitterly if I think that the Quirinale has also appointed those who, however, should amend the Constitution. This was how Piero Calamandrei expressed himself in January 1955: I remember the first injections, after the fall of fascism, on June 6, 1946; this people who for twenty-five years had not enjoyed civil and political freedoms, the first time he went to vote, after a period of horrors, of chaos: civil war, struggles, wars, fires, went to vote. I remember, I was in Florence, the same happened here. These rows of disciplined people in front of the sections. Disciplined and pleased. Because they had the feeling that they had regained their dignity, this giving the vote, this bringing their own opinion to help create, this opinion of the community, this being the masters of us, of our own country, of our homeland, of our land; to dispose us of our destiny, of the destiny of our country. I cannot help but mention these words which are the expression of a highly altruistic, supportive, dignified and democratic thought. We parliamentary citizens of the 5 Star Movement will continue to fight with the weapons that the Constitution itself has given us to defend each of the citizens of the world present in our country. We know that our Constitution remains a marvellous project and that there are countless principles that are still unimplemented that concern respect for the person, his sphere of freedom, his inviolable conscience and his self-determination. In fact, they are not implemented because they speak of weak, humble, workers, students, elderly, children and the sick, but they do not find adequate regulatory answers. You talk about anything else every day, and every day you work not to protect Italian citizens, but to achieve everything that in the Constitution is not, trying to bend it to logics of power that have nothing to do with the spirit with which it was written. But, be careful, I tell you as a citizen who has not stopped being on the street every day listening to the weakest: "A servile democracy where there are those who work without rights is not a democracy." So Gustavo Zagrebelsky pronounced himself. You always speak of development and stability as a prospect to get out of the crisis; but if work is mortified and the humiliated worker no development is possible. The Constitution is an unimplemented project! It must not be changed, it must be achieved in the principles inspired by solidarity. The Constitutional Court is clear: "The principle of solidarity is placed among the founding values of the legal order, so much so that it is solemnly recognized and guaranteed, together with the inviolable rights of man, by Article 2 of the Constitutional Charter as the basis of social coexistence normatively foreshadowed by the constituent." It is from here that we must start again in order to realize a human society: solidarity is within our history and it is together with the work and the protection of the dignity of man. Tahar Ben Jelloun, Moroccan writer said: It is by treating others with dignity that one earns respect for oneself. We believe that this Parliament has not so far had any respect for the dignity of man defended by the Constitution and will never have any title to change it. We citizens of the 5 Star Movement will fight through the inviolable rights that it has granted us to prevent it, not until the end of our mandate but up to the line of our days, for Italy, for the 58 million Italians other than you! [[Applause from the M5S Group]] A brief interruption of the quotation to read Article 2 of the Constitutional Bill which reads: The Committee shall examine the draft Constitutional Review Laws of the Articles referred to in Titles I, II, III and V of Part II of the Constitution. ParlaMint-IT_2013-10-16-LEG17-Senato-sed-126.ana.u67	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I have chosen a number of articles in our Constitution which will show how many times you have been bypassed or ignored and, therefore, why you should not even think that you can change it. Let's start with Article 1, the best known one: Italy is a democratic Republic, founded on labour. Sovereignty belongs to the people, who exercise it in the forms and limits of the Constitution. Article 4. The Republic grants all citizens the right to work and promotes the conditions which make this right effective. Every citizen has the duty to carry out, according to his own possibilities and choice, an activity or function that will contribute to the material or spiritual progress of society. Article 9. The Republic shall promote the development of culture and scientific and technical research. It protects the landscape and the historical and artistic heritage of the Nation. Article 11. Italy repudiates war as an instrument of offense to the freedom of other peoples and as a means of resolving international disputes; it allows, on a level playing field with other States, the limitations of sovereignty necessary for a system that ensures peace and justice between nations; it promotes and favours international organizations aimed at this purpose. Article 21 All have the right to freely manifest their thoughts by word, writing and any other means of dissemination. Printing may not be subject to permission or censorship. The seizure may be carried out only by a reasoned act of the judicial authority in the case of crimes, for which the law on the press expressly authorizes it, or in the case of violation of the rules prescribed by the law itself for the indication of those responsible. (...) Article 32. The Republic protects health as a fundamental right of the individual and the interest of the community, and guarantees free care to the most deprived. No one can be obliged to a certain health treatment except by law. Under no circumstances can the law violate the limits imposed by respect for the human person. Article 33. Art and science are free and free. The Republic shall lay down general rules on education and establish state schools for all orders and grades. Private and private bodies have the right to set up schools and educational institutions, without charge to the State. [[Applause from the M5S Group]] The law, when establishing the rights and obligations of non-state schools applying for equality, must ensure that they have full freedom and that their pupils are treated equally with that of pupils in state schools. A State examination is required for admission to, or for the conclusion of, the various orders and grades of schools and for the admission to professional practice. The institutions of high culture, universities and academies have the right to give themselves autonomous systems within the limits established by the laws of the State. Article 34. The school is open to everyone. Lower education for at least eight years is compulsory and free of charge. Skillful and deserving, even if they lack the means, have the right to reach the highest degrees of study. The Republic shall give effect to this right by means of grants, grants to families and other provisions, which must be awarded by competition. Article 36. The worker is entitled to remuneration proportionate to the quantity and quality of his work and in any case sufficient to ensure that he or she and the family have a free and dignified existence. The maximum duration of the working day is laid down by law. The worker is entitled to weekly rest and paid annual leave and cannot renounce it. Article 37. The female worker has the same rights and, with equal work, the same wages as the worker. Working conditions must allow the fulfilment of its essential family function and provide the mother and the child with special adequate protection. The law lays down the minimum age limit for wage-earners. The Republic shall protect the work of children with special rules and shall guarantee them the right to equal pay for equal work. Article 38. Every citizen unable to work and without the means necessary to live has the right to maintenance and social assistance. Workers are entitled to provide and provide adequate means of living in the event of an accident, illness, invalidity and old age, and involuntary unemployment. The disabled and the disabled have the right to education and vocational training. The tasks provided for in this Article shall be provided by bodies and institutes prepared or supplemented by the State. Private assistance is free. Article 39. The trade union organization is free. No other obligation can be imposed on trade unions other than their registration at local or central offices, in accordance with the law. It is a condition for the registration of the statutes of the unions to establish an internal democratic system. Registered trade unions have legal personality. They may, jointly represented in proportion to their members, conclude collective labour agreements with compulsory effect for all those belonging to the categories to which the contract relates. Article 40. The right to strike is exercised within the framework of the laws governing it. Article 41. The private economic initiative is free. It cannot be carried out in conflict with social usefulness or in such a way as to harm security, freedom and human dignity. The law determines the appropriate programmes and controls so that public and private economic activity can be addressed and coordinated for social purposes. Article 42. The property is public or private. Economic goods belong to the State, to institutions or to private individuals. Private property is recognised and guaranteed by the law, which determines its means of purchase, enjoyment and limits in order to ensure its social function and make it accessible to all. Private property may be, in the cases provided for by law, and subject to compensation, expropriated for reasons of general interest. The law establishes the norms and limits of the legitimate and willary succession and the rights of the State over the inheritances. Article 43. For general purposes, the law may originally reserve or transfer, by expropriation and without compensation, to the State, public bodies or to communities of workers or users certain undertakings or categories of undertakings, which refer to essential public services or to energy sources or to situations of monopoly and which are of a pre-eminent general interest. Article 44. In order to achieve the rational exploitation of the soil and to establish equal social relations, the law imposes obligations and constraints on private land ownership, sets limits to its extension according to the regions and agricultural areas, promotes and imposes the reclamation of the land, the transformation of the land and the rebuilding of the productive units; it helps the small and medium-sized property. The law provides for measures in favour of mountain areas. Article 45. The Republic recognizes the social function of cooperation of a mutual nature and without the purpose of private speculation. The law promotes and promotes the increase with the most suitable means and ensures, with the appropriate controls, its character and purpose. The law provides for the protection and development of crafts. Article 46. For the sake of the economic and social elevation of work and in line with the requirements of production, the Republic recognises the right of workers to cooperate, in the manner and within the limits laid down by law, in the management of companies. Article 47. The Republic encourages and protects savings in all its forms; it regulates, coordinates and controls the exercise of credit. It favours the access of popular savings to the property of the house, to the direct agricultural property and to the direct and indirect investment stock in the great productive complexes of the Country. Article 54. All citizens have a duty to be faithful to the Republic and to observe its Constitution and laws. Citizens entrusted with public functions have a duty to carry out them with discipline and honor, taking an oath in the cases laid down by law. Article 67. Each Member of Parliament represents the nation and performs his duties without a mandate. Article 70. The legislative function is exercised collectively by the two Chambers. I read this article, because maybe it's not very clear to the government: The legislative function is carried out collectively by the two Chambers. [[Applause from the M5S Group]] Article 76. The exercise of the legislative function cannot be delegated to the Government except with determination of guiding principles and criteria and only for limited time and for defined objects. Article 77. The Government cannot, without delegation of the Chambers, issue decrees that have the value of ordinary law. When, in extraordinary cases of necessity and urgency, the Government adopts, under its responsibility, provisional measures by force of law, it must present them on the same day for conversion to the Chambers which, even if dissolved, are specially convened and meet within five days. The decrees lose effect from the beginning, if they are not converted into law within sixty days of their publication. However, the Chambers can regulate with law the legal relations arising on the basis of non-converted decrees. Article 85. The President of the Republic is elected for seven years. Thirty days before the end of the term, the President of the Chamber of Deputies shall convene in common session the Parliament and regional delegates, to elect the new President of the Republic. If the Rooms are dissolved, or less than three months after their termination, the election shall take place within fifteen days of the meeting of the New Rooms. In the meantime, the powers of the President-in-Office are extended. Article 87. The President of the Republic is the Head of State and represents national unity. You can send messages to the Rooms. Indicate the elections of the new Chambers and set the first meeting. It authorizes the presentation to the Houses of the Government's own-initiative drafts of law. Promulgate laws and enact decrees of law value and regulations. Index the popular referendum in the cases provided for in the Constitution. Appointment, in the cases indicated by law, of officials of the State. Accredits and receives diplomatic representatives, ratifys international treaties, after, when necessary, the authorization of the Chambers. He's in command of the Armed Forces. presides over the Supreme Council of Defense constituted according to the law, declares the state of war deliberated by the Chambers; The Supreme Council of the judiciary presides. It can grant grace and commute the penalties. It bestows the honours of the Republic. Article 98. The public employees are at the nation's exclusive service. If they are Members of Parliament, they cannot achieve promotions except for seniority. It is possible by law to establish limitations on the right to join political parties for magistrates, career soldiers in active service, officials and police officers, diplomatic and consular representatives abroad. Title IV. The Magistrate. Section I. Judicial order. Article 101. Justice is administered in the name of the people. Judges shall be subject only to the law of the Court of Justice. And I read this again: The courts are subject only to the law of the Court of Justice. [[Applause from the M5S Group.]] Article 102. The judicial function shall be exercised by ordinary judges established and governed by the rules on the judicial order. No special Judges or special Judges may be established. They can only be established in ordinary judicial bodies specialised in certain matters, including with the participation of suitable citizens outside the judiciary. The law regulates the cases and forms of direct participation of the people in the administration of justice. Article 103. The Council of State and the other administrative justice bodies have jurisdiction to protect legitimate interests vis-à-vis the public administration and, in particular matters indicated by law, also subjective rights. The Court of Auditors shall have jurisdiction in matters of public accounting and other matters specified by law. Military courts in wartime have jurisdiction established by law. In peacetime they have jurisdiction only for military crimes committed by members of the Armed Forces. Article 104. The judiciary is an autonomous and independent order from any other power. And here too I have to reread: the judiciary is an autonomous and independent order from any other power. [[Applause from the M5S Group]] The Supreme Council of the judiciary is chaired by the President of the Republic. The first President and the Attorney General of the Court of Cassation are in law members of the Court of Cassation. The other members are elected two thirds by all ordinary judges from among the various categories, and one third by Parliament in common session between ordinary university professors in legal matters and lawyers after fifteen years of exercise. The Council shall elect a vice-president from among the members appointed by Parliament. The elected members of the Council shall remain in office for four years and shall not be re-elected immediately. They cannot, as long as they are in office, be registered in the professional register or be a member of Parliament or of a Regional Council. Article 105. It is the responsibility of the Supreme Council of the judiciary, in accordance with the rules of the judiciary, recruitment, assignments and transfers, promotions and disciplinary measures with regard to judges. Article 106. The appointment of magistrates shall take place by competition. The law on the judicial order may allow the appointment, even elective, of honorary magistrates for all duties assigned to individual judges. Upon designation of the Supreme Council of the judiciary may be called to the office of councilors of cassation, for outstanding merits, ordinary university professors in legal matters and lawyers who are fifteen years old and are registered in the special offices for higher courts. Article 107. The magistrates are immovable. They may not be waived or suspended from the service or intended for other offices or functions except as a result of a decision of the Supreme Council of the judiciary, adopted either on the grounds and with the guarantees of defence established by the judicial order or with their consent. The Minister of Justice shall have the power to promote disciplinary action. The magistrates distinguish between themselves only because of different functions. The public prosecutor shall enjoy the guarantees established in respect of him by the rules on the judicial order. Article 108. The rules on the judicial system and on each judiciary are laid down by law. The law ensures the independence of judges of special courts, prosecutors, and outsiders participating in the administration of justice. Article 109. The judicial authority shall have direct access to the judicial police. Article 110. Without prejudice to the powers of the Supreme Council of the judiciary, the Minister of Justice shall be responsible for the organisation and operation of justice services. Section II. "Rules of jurisdiction" is replaced by "Rules of jurisdiction." Article 111. Jurisdiction is carried out through the right process governed by law.
